[Table_Title] 
假期扰动 PMI走势，内外需双双放缓

[Table_ReportType] 
——10月PMI数据点评

[Table_ReportDate] 2026年11月02日

[Table_Summary] ⚫ 事件： 10月中采制造业PMI为 49.3%，较上月下跌0.8个
百分点，非制造业PMI由50.2%下跌至49.7%。 
⚫ 制造业PMI环比季节性回落，中小企业景气跌幅较大。PMI
再度降至临界值下方，工作天数减少带来一定影响。中、小
型企业PMI回落幅度较大，均处于临近值下方，大型企业
PMI小幅回落，仍处于景气区间。 
⚫ 产需双双回落，进出口景气度降温，价格指标景气度回落。
生产指数降至临界值下方，新订单指数下跌，生产回落快于
需求。新出口订单、进口指数下跌，而欧美日等制造业PMI
景气度整体保持旺盛。企业采购意愿下滑，生产放缓影响企
业采购需求。纺织、有色金属、非金属矿等行业生产、新订
单偏弱。原材料价格回落幅度大于出厂价格跌幅，中下游企
业成本压力边际缓解，有色金属板块价格跌幅较大，能耗产
业链价格维持高景气。原材料库存累积、产成品库存去化的
趋势持续，主动补库存周期尚不稳固。 
⚫ 高技术、装备制造高位放缓，消费品、基础原材料、钢铁行
业景气度下滑。新经济景气度高位回落，但仍处景气区间，
EPMI大幅拉升，供需持续好转，秋季旺季特征明显，采购
价格持续上行，但速度放缓。SMI中枢上行，中上游制造业
显著修复。消费品行业PMI重回荣枯线下方，双节备货拉动
消退后，内需接续效应不足。兰格钢铁PMI下滑幅度较大，
销售价格和采购成本大幅回落，供需均转弱。 
⚫ 建筑业为非制造业PMI主要拖累，服务业PMI处于临界点。
非制造业PMI新订单和新出口订单指数下滑幅度较大，需求
持续偏弱，价格指标回落。其中，建筑业PMI下滑至临界值
下方，工作日减少带来一定影响，新订单指数跌幅较大，建
筑业销售价格上行，投入品价格回落，钢材价格下跌形成一
定拖累。服务业PMI小幅放缓，新订单指数跌幅较大，价格
指数回落，仍处景气区间，十一长假给价格带来支撑。 
⚫ 季节性因素干扰PMI表现，政策扶持下四季度经济仍有支
撑。10月受长假和外部扰动等影响，PMI环比承压，内外需
均放缓。BCI企业利润、销售前瞻指数小幅回落，销售前瞻
指数维持在60以上，仍具韧性，油价上行给利润带来一定
压力，融资环境指数小幅上行。随着财政资金逐步落地，有
望加快形成实物工作量，带动基建、制造业投资好转。 
⚫ 风险因素：地缘政治风险发展超预期，政策出台和落地不及
预期，外需回落幅度超预期。

采矿业为利润主要拉动，企业库存持续累积 
财政支出有望继续提速

万联证券研究所 www.wlzq.cn 第 3 页 共 7 页  
事件 
2026年10月，中采制造业PMI为49.3%， 较上月下跌0.8个百分点， 非制造业PMI为49.7%，
较上月下跌0.5个百分点，综合PMI为49.7%，环比下跌1个百分点。

1    PMI环比季节性回落，产需双双放缓 
制造业PMI环比季节性回落，中小企业景气回落幅度较大。制造业PMI环比回落0.8个
百分点至49.3%，再度降至临界值下方，工作天数减少带来一定影响，财政投放提速，
项目建设给PMI表现形成支撑。从不同规模企业来看，小型企业PMI下行1.4个百分点
至47.5%， 中型企业PMI下行1.3个百分点至48.4%， 大型企业PMI由50.6%回落至50.2%，
仅大型企业处于景气区间，中小企业依然承压。 
高技术、装备制造高位继续回落，战略新兴产业显著回暖，消费品、基础原材料、钢
铁行业景气度下滑。高技术制造业继续下行1.2个百分点至51.3%，装备制造业由51%
回落为50.6%，高端制造业景气度持续回落，但仍处景气区间。基础原材料行业由48%
回落为47.3%。消费品行业下行1.9个百分点至48.8%，重回荣枯线下方，双节备货拉
动消退后，内需接续效应不足。战略性新兴产业PMI（EPMI）由52.3%拉升至58%，达
到年内高点，其中，生产量、产品订货和进口指数上行幅度较大，供需双双好转，秋
季旺季特征明显， 但进口指数仍处荣枯线下方， 采购价格持续上行， 但速度明显放缓，
就业和研发指标也有所上行。光伏经理人指数（SMI）10月中枢较9月上行，中上游制
造业显著修复，下游电站小幅回落。兰格钢铁PMI由49.8%回落为46.9%，下滑幅度较
大，其中，销售价格和采购成本大幅回落，下行至荣枯线下方，销量、总订单、国内
订单也明显回落，出口订单量小幅下滑，仅库存水平环比上行。

图表1：10月制造业PMI环比季节性回落（%） 图表2：高技术制造、装备制造业PMI继续回落（%）

资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所 资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所

口指数环比转为下跌2.2个百分点至47%。采购量下行2.1个百分点至48.9%，生产放缓
影响企业采购需求回落。供货商配送时间由50.1%上行至50.2%。从统计局公布数据来
看，铁路船舶航空航天设备、计算机通信电子设备等行业生产指数和新订单指数均位
于53.0%及以上；纺织、非金属矿物制品、有色金属冶炼及压延加工等行业生产指数
和新订单指数均低于临界点，地产持续偏弱对上游的拖累作用持续，部分有色金属价
格、库存偏高给产需带来一定影响。美国制造业PMI高位边际放缓，但景气度仍强，
日本制造业PMI持续小幅回落，仍处荣枯线上方。德国、英国制造业PMI上行，处于临
界点上方，法国受国内动乱影响，制造业PMI降至荣枯线下方。 
图表3：供需分项环比回落，价格指标下行（%） 图表4：美国制造业PMI小幅回落，景气仍强（%）

资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所 资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所 
价格指标景气度回落，原材料和加工行业剪刀差收窄，有色行业价格下滑为主要拖
累，能化产业链维持高景气。主要原材料购进价格回落4个百分点至56.8%，出厂价格
下行2.3个百分点至51.7%，原材料价格回落幅度大于出厂价格跌幅，中下游企业成本
压力边际缓解。10月，受到宏观流动性收紧预期和基本面供需矛盾等因素影响，有色
金属板块承压，金、银、钴、碳酸锂等有色金属跌幅较大。国际油价持续攀升，石油
煤炭及其他燃料加工、化学纤维及橡胶塑料制品等行业价格指数维持高景气。 
原材料库存累积、产成品库存去化的趋势持续，主动补库存周期尚不稳固。产成品库
存由47.6%下跌至47.5%， 原材料库存上行0.2个百分点至48.4%。原料端价格压力持续，
企业备库需求和价格增长带动原材料库存上行。生产经营活动预期指标由53.8%回落
为53.4%。 
图表5：原材料库存累积，产成品库存去化（%） 图表6：原材料价格和出厂价格价差明显收窄（%）

2    非制造业PMI回落，建筑业为主要拖累 
非制造业PMI回落，需求持续偏弱，价格指标回落。10月非制造业PMI为49.7%，较上
月下跌0.5个百分点。其中，新订单和新出口订单指数下滑幅度较大，持续处于临近
值下方，在手订单指数也有所回落。商务活动指数下滑，降至临近值下方。投入品价
格回落幅度大于销售价格回落幅度。业务活动预期由55.5%回落至54.7%，企业经营预
期下跌，但仍处景气区间。仅从业人员由45.9%小幅上行至46%。 
建筑业PMI下滑至临界值下方，新订单指数下滑幅度较大，工作日减少带来一定影响，
销售价格和投入品价格走势分化。建筑业PMI下跌至48%，上月为50.3%。其中，建筑
业新订单指数下跌5.7个百分点至40%， 建筑业业务活动预期下跌3.1个百分点至52.3%，
建筑业销售价格上行，投入品价格回落，钢材价格下跌形成一定拖累。随着财政资金
落地， “六张网”建设逐步显效，有望带动建筑业回暖。 
服务业PMI处于临界点，新订单指数持续偏弱，价格指数回落。服务业PMI为50%，较
上月下跌0.2个百分点。服务业新订单指数下跌，回落幅度相对最大。服务业投入品
和销售价格均下跌，仍处景气区间，十一长假给价格带来支撑。业务活动预期指数小
幅回落，仍处临界值上方。从统计局公布数据来看，电信广播电视及卫星传输服务、
货币金融服务、保险等行业商务活动指数持续位于55.0%以上的高景气区间；资本市
场服务、房地产等行业商务活动指数低位运行。

图表7：建筑业PMI新订单跌幅较大（%） 图表8：服务业新订单下跌(%)

资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所 资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所

制造业用工需求持平，服务业就业好转，建筑业就业回落。10月制造业PMI从业人员
持平于上月的48.4%。服务业从业人员较上月上行0.5个百分点至46.7%，建筑业从业
人员指数为42%，较上月下跌2.3个百分点，十一长假影响建筑业用工需求，给服务业
就业带来提振。

3    总结与展望 
季节性因素干扰PMI表现，政策扶持下四季度经济仍有支撑。10月受长假和外部扰动
等影响，PMI环比承压，内外需均放缓。BCI企业利润、销售前瞻指数小幅回落，销售
前瞻指数维持在60以上，仍具韧性，油价上行给利润带来一定压力，融资环境指数小
幅上行。随着财政资金逐步落地，有望加快形成实物工作量，带动基建、制造业投资
好转。

图表9：企业利润、销售前瞻指数小幅回落（%） 图表10：企业融资环境指数小幅上行（%）

资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所 资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所

4    风险提示 
地缘政治风险发展超预期，政策出台和落地不及预期，外需回落幅度超预期。

[Table_InudstryRankInfo] 行业投资评级 
强于大市：未来6个月内行业指数相对大盘涨幅10％以上； 
同步大市：未来6个月内行业指数相对大盘涨幅10％至-10％之间； 
弱于大市：未来6个月内行业指数相对大盘跌幅10％以上。

[Table_StockRankInfo] 公司投资评级 
买入：未来6个月内公司相对大盘涨幅15％以上； 
增持：未来6个月内公司相对大盘涨幅5％至15％； 
观望：未来6个月内公司相对大盘涨幅-5％至5％； 
卖出：未来6个月内公司相对大盘跌幅5％以上。 
基准指数 ：沪深300指数

[Table_RiskInfo] 风险提示 
我们在此提醒您，不同证券研究机构采用不同的评级术语及评级标准。我们采用的是相对评级体系，表示投资的
相对比重建议；投资者买入或者卖出证券的决定取决于个人的实际情况，比如当前的持仓结构以及其他需要考
虑的因素。投资者应阅读整篇报告，以获取比较完整的观点与信息，不应仅仅依靠投资评级来推断结论。

出具本报告。本报告清晰准确地反映了本人的研究观点。本人不曾因，不因，也将不会因本报告中的具体推荐意
见或观点而直接或间接收到任何形式的补偿。

### Page 2

足許の経済・金融状況と注目ポイント
1
（注）予想はLSEG、ブルームバーグが取りまとめた予想コンセンサス
（出所）LSEG、ブルームバーグより、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
2026. 11.2
先週（10/26～10/30）発表の主な経済指標
AI関連を中心に設備投資が増勢を維持し、産業用機械にも広
がり。好調な企業収益も投資を後押し。個人消費も堅調。金
融所得や移転所得、株高による資産効果が購買力を下支え
政策要因を除く都区部CPIは上昇基調。財価格の上昇が目立
つ一方、サービス価格改定の広がりは限定的。企業の価格転嫁
は進展も、消費マインドの弱さが値上げの重荷に

米国：企業収益が拡大する一方、労働分配率は低下。金融所得や移転所得が消費を下支え
 企業収益が好調を維持する一方、労働所得の対GDP比は低下傾向。金融所得や政府からの移転所得が家計所得を補完

日本：コアCPIは10月に鈍化も、政策要因を除けばインフレ率は上昇基調
4
 10月の都区部コアＣＰＩは前年比＋2.6％（前月：同＋2.7％）と伸び鈍化
― 無償化期間の時期ズレによる9月水道料金の一時的な押し上げが剥落したことに加え、コメ価格の下落が影響
 ただし、政策要因を除けばインフレ率は上昇基調。サービス物価が底堅く推移する中、財物価の加速がインフレ率押し上げ
― 原油高等によるコスト増の転嫁が進展し、エネルギー、その他財（除く水道料金）の価格上昇が足元で加速
― 春先に鈍化した一般サービス物価（除く家賃）も人件費等の転嫁進展を映じて前年比＋2％近傍で推移

―10月は外食を中心に価格改定の動きがみられるも、広がりは限定的。一方、外食除く改定品目では値上げ幅が拡大

日本：企業の価格転嫁は進展も、消費マインドの弱さが追加値上げの重荷に
5
 企業の価格転嫁は着実に進展
― 日商LOBO調査では、コスト増加分の4割以上を価格転嫁できた企業が55.4％と4月から2.6％ポイント増加。ただし、労
務費の転嫁は40.6％にとどまり、販売単価DIも小幅低下するなど、中小企業では追加値上げの勢いに一服感
 消費者態度指数は2カ月連続で悪化。消費マインドの弱さは残存
― 物価高による節約志向が企業の追加値上げを制約している可能性。日銀は基調的な物価上昇率が2％に到達したと判
断し、上振れリスクへの警戒を強化。ただし、サービス価格の改定に広がりはみられず
（出所） 日本商工会議所「LOBO調査」より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

米国：10月FOMC～据え置き、ウォーシュ議長は引き締めがAIセクターにも影響との認識示す
6
 3.75～4.00％に据え置き（2人が反対、利上げを支持）。景気評価を上方修正しつつ、9月利上げ効果を見極め
― 景気評価は「堅調」から「力強い」へ。ウォーシュ議長は投資の広がりに言及
 AIセクターでは投資の選別が起きており、高い成長期待を持つ同セクターに対しても金融政策は有効との認識示す
 2027・2028年の会合の一部を政策決定を行わず、声明文も公表しない戦略的議論の会合とすると表明
AIセクター、生産性、タスクフォース声明文の変更、政策判断、会合運営
 反対2票は利上げを主張
 景気評価は「堅調」から「力強い」へ
 様子見による「情報のさらなる蓄積」
が、物価安定への「適時の復帰」のため
の政策判断に役立つ
声明文
 「以前より引き締まった」が「引き締め
的な水準にあるわけではない」と述べ、
追加利上げの余地残す
 「参加者の多数派は利上げ効果が出てい
ると認識」と言及

金融環境
と効果
 「政治は、この決定に何の役割も果たし
ていない」と言明 中間選挙
 2027年秋の1回、2028年には2回の会合
を政策決定せず議論だけの会合へ
 戦略的な熟議のための時間と余地確保
会合運営
 「投資家が期待する収益率はあまりに違
うので、AIには何の影響も及ばない」と
いう見方を否定
 「新技術と今回の投資急増」に対して金

 今後数四半期の物価は目標を大幅に上回る見通し。その後
はエネルギー価格低下と金利上昇により目標水準に回帰へ

期段階では上昇圧力がみられる
 今後6週間で得られる、基調的なインフレに関する多くの情
報を、とりわけ注視していく

 9月理事会以降、金融環境は引き締まっている。金利は上昇
し、株価は下落している
 期待インフレの上昇やハイパースケーラーの社債発行等、

欧州：ECBは政策金利を据え置き。高金利は企業の資金需要を押し下げ
7
 ECBは預金ファシリティ金利を2.5％で据え置き。利上げが議論されるも物価の基調に著変なく、慎重な対応が適切と判断
― 利上げの要否検討にあたり、エネルギー価格の上昇と金融環境の引き締まりが主な論点に。今後は物価の基調に関す
る指標を特に注視する姿勢。ラガルド総裁は、進むべき方向が「きわめて明確」であると発言
― ラガルド総裁は2027年2月に早期退任へ。同年5月までに専務理事2名を含む計3名の幹部が交代する見込み
 10月の銀行貸出調査によれば、金利水準が企業の資金需要減少に寄与。高金利の影響は既に一部で顕在化
― 同調査によれば、企業向け貸出基準はフランスで一段と厳格化。銀行のリスク許容度低下やリスク認識の高まりが主因

企業向け銀行貸出の需要増減要因
（注） 需要増減は、企業向け貸出の需要が前四半期から「かなり増加」「やや増加」と回
答した銀行の割合から、「かなり減少」「やや減少」と回答した銀行の割合を引いた
もの。「その他要因」には他の調達手段による代替や、「運転資金・在庫」「設備投
資」以外の資金需要発生等を含む。2026年Q3の調査期間は9月21日～10月6日
（出所）ECBより、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
10月ECB理事会のポイント（声明文・総裁会見）
（出所）ECBより、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

### Page 9

英国：政府は財政規律を重視しつつエネルギー高対策に注力。財政余力は縮小
 英政府は秋季財政計画を公表。財政規律を重視しつつ家計や小規模事業者向け支援等を打ち出す内容
― 燃料税減税と電気料金へのVAT減税の延長、給付金支給によるエネルギー高対策、住宅購入支援などの家計支援策
を発表。小規模事業者の税負担軽減や電気料金の引き下げなど、企業向け支援も公表
― 上記政策の財源は銀行への追加課税やギャンブルへの課税強化等で確保する方針
― 防衛費は2030年までにGDP比3％へ引き上げる方針。ただし、財源を含む詳細は来年の歳出見直しに先送り
 財政余力は前回3月の237億ポンドから147億ポンドに大幅縮小。中東情勢悪化に伴う金利上昇等が背景
英国：財政余力（左）と3月以降の財政余力減少要因（右）
（出所）OBRより、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
英国：秋季財政計画の主な内容
（出所） 英国政府より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
• 燃料税5ペンス減税や電気料金のVA T免除を来年7月まで実施
• 2,000 万世帯を対象に100ポンドのエネルギー高支援給付金支給

• 使用量にかかわらず課される家庭用ガスのスタンディングチャー
ジ廃止
• エネルギー集約型製造業1万社の電気代を20～25％軽減
• 手ごろな住宅建設に15億ポンド追加拠出。住宅初回購入者向け
支援「Your First Home」に47億ポンドを充当
• 小規模事業者の事業用不動産税免除対象を拡大。パブや音楽会
場等には同税を追加で20％軽減
• 食品等100件以上の品目の関税撤廃
• 防衛投資計画で必要とされていた150億ポンドの追加投資全額を
拠出。2030年に防衛費GDP比3％達成を企図。来年の歳出見直
しで財源の詳細公表
• 地方分権の一環として、来年から事業用不動産税のうち20％を地
方に移転、2030年までに100％への引き上げを目指すほか、所得
税も2028年から一部を地方に配分しインフラ整備の財源に
• 銀行への上乗せ課税を3％から6％に引き上げ
• 高額賭博機へのギャンブル税を20％から25％に引き上げ
• 貯蓄・不動産所得の税率を2％Pt引き上げ

英国：財政懸念を背景とする金利上昇リスクはくすぶり続ける見通し
 財政計画がおおむね事前の予想どおりの内容であったことなどから英10年債利回りの動きは限定的。ただし、足元の高金
利の要因であるインフレや財政懸念、世界的なAI関連投資増加は今後も続くとみられ、金利は高止まりが予想される

 利払い費による財政圧迫継続が予想される中、財政懸念を背景とする一段の金利上昇リスクはくすぶり続ける見通し
― 高金利継続による利払い負担が財政を圧迫。財政を巡る不確実性が高い状況は続く見通し
― 欧州各国では財政懸念や政情不安を背景に高債務国中心に国債利回り上昇
― 財政への懸念を払拭しにくい状況が続くなか、英国でも金利急騰とこれに伴う財政悪化リスクには要注意
政府債務残高と10年債利回り変化幅の関係

中国：製造業利益は引き続きAI需要と資源高がけん引。7～9月期の利益率は前期を下回る
 9月の製造業総利益は前年比＋14.4％と前月（同＋9.1％）から加速。引き続きAI需要と資源高がけん引
― コンピューター・通信機器は同＋72.9％の増益。石油・石炭加工は単月赤字から一転し、同10倍超の伸び
― 他方、鉄鋼は単月赤字に転落したほか、自動車も同▲68.5％の減益。過剰生産業種は厳しい状況
 7～9月期の製造業の売上高利益率は5.0％と4～6月期（5.5％）から低下。自動車など幅広い業種で前期を下回る
― コンピューター・通信機器は7.4％で、前年（4.3％）から急速に改善。資源高で非鉄金属加工も好調

― 他方、自動車は2.5％と、前年（3.8％）から大幅に悪化。過当競争による販売価格の低下が響いた格好

アジア：韓国・台湾では供給能力の拡大に向け、半導体産業の設備投資が活発化
 半導体生産は台湾で拡大が継続も、韓国ではほぼ横ばい。供給能力の限界により増産が抑制されている可能性
― 韓国での供給制約は、AIサーバー等に使用される半導体メモリの需給ひっ迫、価格高騰の要因に

― 韓国、台湾の主要な半導体・電子部品企業は、生産能力増強のため大規模な設備増強投資を計画
― 足元では韓国、台湾ともに半導体産業の機械設備投資が拡大傾向。主要各社による投資計画の進捗を示唆

 中東情勢緊迫化後、食料や燃料を中心にインフレ率が急上昇したタイとフィリピンでは、4～6月期に消費の伸びが減速
― タイ、フィリピンとも生活必需財である飲食料品の消費は維持
― タイでは燃料を中心とする交通費、フィリピンでは交通関連に加え、宿泊飲食なども抑制する形で消費者が節約行動
 背景に実質賃金の鈍化による購買力の低下
― 購買力低下を受け、タイは6月～11月に消費支援策（食品・日用品などの購入補助）を実施、フィリピンは政府による企
業への補助も伴いながら首都圏の最低賃金の大幅な引き上げを6月に発令。物価上昇への対応が両国の政治課題に

日本：基調的物価は2％到達も安定化には距離。日銀は利上げの影響を見極め微妙な舵取りへ
13
日銀10月会合のポイント（展望レポート・総裁会見）
（出所）日本銀行「経済・物価情勢の展望」より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
 日銀は基調的物価について「2％目標と概ね整合的な水準」に評価変更も、10月会合では利上げを見送り
― 今後は物価を2％で安定化させる局面へ。金融環境は緩和的との評価を維持し、次回12月会合で追加利上げの公算大
― 経済・物価はオントラックとの評価。原油価格の想定上振れを一部反映も見通し修正は軽微
― 総裁会見では「直近で物価上振れリスクが高まったわけではない」との発言。一段の利上げ加速は喫緊の必要性なし
 利上げを通じた基調的物価への影響を見極める手がかりとして、日銀は金融環境を注視

― 銀行・企業向けアンケート調査や銀行貸出残高にみられる変化、グローバル金融市場からの影響波及には要注意
経済・物価見通し
（注） 青字＝上昇修正、赤字＝下方修正。ドバイ原油価格は1バレル105ドル程度を出発
点に70ドル程度まで下落していくと前提。飲食料品消費税率の引き下げの影響は
織り込まず
（出所） 日本銀行より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
全体として修正は軽微。日銀見通し
対比でオントラックとの評価
展望レポート
• 基調的物価上昇率は2%物価目標と概ね整合的な水準
• 実質金利は上昇しているが、短期中心になお低水準。資金需要、
資産市場動向等も踏まえると金融環境は依然緩和した状態
• 名目賃金は前年並みのベースアップが実現するもとで、着実な増
加が続く見込み
• 海外の多くの中央銀行が利上げ方向に政策を転換。グローバルな
金融環境の変化がわが国の経済・物価に及ぼす影響に留意
会見中の総裁発言
• 物価上振れリスクはなおも無視できないが、1カ月前から物価上振
れリスクは大きく高まったわけではない

• 基調的物価が2%にアンカーされている度合いは極めて低い
• 金融政策が適時適切に発動され、緩和度合いを調整していくこと
で期待が安定化されていく
• 本支店ヒアリングでは、中小零細を中心に資金繰りがタイトになり
つつあるという声が聞かれ、今後の広がりを注意深くみていく
• 早めの政策対応を基本方針とするべき状況までは至っていない

日本：AI関連財の増産を背景に設備投資は今後拡大も、輸入依存の高さが成長の抑制要因に
14
 9月鉱工業生産は前月比＋2.2％と3カ月ぶりに増産。26年に入ってから増産基調を維持
― 増産の主因はAI関連。旺盛なAI関連需要を背景に、半導体製造装置などの増産が鉱工業生産をけん引
― 加えて、自動車生産の持ち直しなど、熊本地震や豪雨による下振れからの回復も生産の押し上げに寄与
 AI関連を中心に増産基調の生産動向は、今後の設備投資拡大を示唆
― ただし、資本財の輸入依存度は上昇傾向。AI関連業種で設備不足感が強まるなか、資本財の供給を輸入に頼る構造
― 人手不足などの供給制約で国内メーカーの増産が追いつかず、拡大する投資需要の一部が海外に流出の可能性も

世界経済はどこへ向かうのか？中東危機やAI・レジリエンス関連の投資が
もたらす変化や、経済・金融市場の行方を展望
【ポイント①】
中東危機が⾧期化するなかでも、AI・レジリエンス関連の旺盛な投資需要に
支えられ、世界経済は3％台の成⾧ペースを維持
【ポイント②】
世界はインフレ・高金利の新常態へ。成⾧・負担の偏在が経済主体間の
「耐性格差」を拡大
【ポイント③】
海外主要中銀はインフレ警戒から利上げモードへ、日銀も物価上振れを警戒

volume on the most high end M8.5/M9 CCL, we now expect EMC’s revenue to go up by
20% QoQ, and expect the GM will further expand to 40.9% (first time surpassing
40%).
For TUC, with an even faster product mix improvement speed (due to the lower base vs.

EMC) and the solid pricing hike uptrend for both high end and mid end CCL (15-20%
QoQ average pricing hike in 4Q), we expect the company to deliver a revenue growth
rate at 18% QoQ, and believe the GM will reach 40.8% (+3.9ppt QoQ) in 4Q26,
despite the slowing demand from key AI ASIC customer due to the transition period of
the ASIC projects whose capacity will be replaced by other high-end Ethernet switch
projects (also M8.5/M9 grade CCL).
Key update - EMC now plan to expand capacity to 15.25mn sheets/month from

5.85mn sheets/month capacity today, with solid demand
EMC’s BOD has approved additional capacity expansion plans extending into
2029/30E. EMC now targets year-end capacity of 8.70/10.45/11.95/15.25mn
sheets/month in 2027/28/29/30E (implying 27% 2026-30E CAGR), which is the first
time EMC announced its new capacity expansion plan after 1H28. EMC mentioned that
capacity expansion is supported by LTAs with customers who secure volumes (EMC does
not proactively initiate LTA negotiations). EMC indicated that despite its proactive

capacity expansion planned for 2027-30E, it would still be insufficient to meet with
customer demand secured under signed LTAs (demand under LTA extends at least 3Y
ahead).
EMC’s near-term capacity expansion remains intact, as the capacity expansion in
Kunshan (with additional 1.2mn sheets/month, 21% of current capacity) could
come online as early as Dec 2026 and continue through May 2027 (with new capacity
coming online every month), the expansion was originally scheduled in 2 phases, with

600k sheets/month each coming online in 1Q27 and 3Q27. The accelerated ramp-up
will offset the rescheduling of Guanyin capacity expansion, where the 300k sheet/month
expansions originally scheduled for 4Q26 have been deferred to 2H27, mainly due to
shortages of construction capacity in Taiwan as well as long equipment lead time +
Taiwan government’s strict policy on labor safety related requirement (however, we only
see delay in Taiwan, while all other plans are basically in-line or earlier than the

company’s original expectation).
Investment view - Solid margin beat + expanding margin, suggesting a stock
performance outlook; buy in the dip for both EMC & TUC
We notice that the BBG consensus for 2027 EMC/TUC GM is only at 38%/34%, and we
see upside risk to consensus EPS estimates for both EMC/TUC as the GM in 4Q could
both surpass 40-42%, without any possibility to see the GM to decrease in coming
quarters as the high-end CCL supply demand gap to further expand in coming years (see

here). As a result, we believe consensus 2027 GM could lift from 34-38% to 40-42%+ for
both companies in coming days, suggesting 10-25% EPS upward revision opportunity,
and recommend taking advantage to buy in this valuation dip (EMC/TUC only trades at
15x/12x of our bullish 2027 EPS estimate, which was far below the past 3 year range of

20-30x).  
Moreover, EMC has mentioned all of its new capacity in coming years (capacity will 
expand by 2.6x in 2030 vs. 2026; see more details above) is still not large enough to 
support customers’ LTAs volume demand, suggesting an even more favorable high-end 
CCL pricing outlook in coming years (all the way to 2030), which is in-line with what we 
discussed in our TPCA takeaway report. We expect EMC/TUC will rise the like for like 
base pricing for M7+ CCL by 10-40%, and we now expect the similar pricing hike ratio in 
2027/28, with more room to adjust if the supply shortage issue continues.  
For the new CCL generation, we exect EMC to start shipment on M10 grade CCL from 
2H27, which is only for single customers’ AI GPU projects, but still implies a CCL 
migration trend in-line with our bullish view. For M9, we only see HGX R200 UBB and 
VR200 LPU adopt this solution in 2026, but see multiple new projects in 2027 will adopt 
this high ASP project (including several 1.6T ethernet switch projects, and Trainium 4/ 
TPU v9 could also see at least 50% adoption rate of the M9 CCL; moreover, the VR300 
rack should have 50%+ of its CCL content related to M9 grade CCL). We believe this new 
technology migration trend going into 2027 will further drive both companies’ ASP and 
profit in coming quarters, suggesting an even more favorable outlook for EMC/TUC’s EPS 
growth in coming years.  
Overall, we maintain our bullish view on high-end CCL players’ business outlook and 
margin expansion trend, and expect investors to start focusing on this solid margin 
expansion trend after this clean beat on GM/OPM in 3Q26; maintain Buy on 
EMC/TUC, with a new TP of NT$11,700/NT$3,460 (from NT$10,200/NT$3,010).

3-5+ years. We are positive on its market share trajectory in the server industry and

Price Target Risks and Methodology - Elite Material  
Valuation methodology: Our 12m TP of NT$11,700 is based on 27x 2H27-1H28E P/E 
(in line with the peak P/E multiple for AI component suppliers in the past 3 years).  
Key downside risks:  (1) RCC to replace all high-end smartphone HDI design; (2) rising 
trade tensions, which could lead to weaker smartphone and server shipments; and (3) 
rising competition from mainland China peers. 
Investment Thesis - Taiwan Union Technology Corp. 
TUC is a key high-end global CCL supplier that focuses on M7+ grade high-speed CCL 
(key applications include high-end switches (100G+) and AI server materials), with 20%+ 
market share in the past two years. The company continues to expect its unit market 
share in the server industry to increase from 15% in Whitley and Purley processors to 
higher in the Eagle Stream generation, and plans to launch two types of products for 
low-end and high-end customers (T2A and T2C) to gain market share. Also, strong 
growth in 400G/800G switch shipments and new 800G switch shipments, scheduled for 
launch in 2025-27, should continue to be a key demand driver for TUC, in our view. 
Additionally, the company is working on high-end AI projects with CSP and enterprise 
players, which we believe should drive revenue momentum in the long term. We view the 
stock as undervalued vs. Taiwan CCL peers on a P/E basis and rate it Buy.  
Key downside risks include: (1) slower-than-expected share gains in the low-loss CCL 
segment; (2) rising trade tensions, which could lead to weaker server and switch 
shipments globally; and (3) rising competition from mainland China peers.

Price Target Risks and Methodology - Taiwan Union Technology Corp. 
Valuation methodology: Our 12m TP of NT$3,460 is based on a 22x 2H27-1H28E P/E 
(+2x STDV higher than the past 3-year industry average P/E multiple).

Document 125: 261029_daiwa_Win Semiconductors

Date: 2026-10-29

Status: ok

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: Following our results memo, we raise our forecasts and TP 
for Win Semiconductors (WinSemi), but lower our rating by one notch to 
Outperform (2) on valuation grounds. While its topline trajectory appears to 
be unfolding intact, its margin trajectory is running better than our 
expectations. This, together with management’s positive commentary 
across its key business lines of RF, infrastructure and optics, reinforces our 
thesis that 3 growth pillars will turn around WinSemi’s bottom line and 
optimise its structural profitability (Upgrading: profit optimisation round the 
corner, 24 July). We suggest accumulating only on pullbacks.

What’s the impact: Forecast revisions. We raise our 2027-28E EPS by 
2-11% for WinSemi to factor in its higher margin trajectory, primarily on 
better product mix geared towards the infrastructure business, followed by 
the ramp-up in optics for AI optical communications (OC). We note the 
surge in our 2026E EPS revision is a result of the company’s non-operating 
gains of TWD684m in 3Q26, mainly from its 23%-owned ITEQ (6213 TT, 
TWD778, Buy [1], covered by Sheng Cheng), which distorts WinSemi’s 
growth trajectory at the bottom line into 2027.

Transformation paying off. At its results call, management commented on 
resilient RF demand from iOS and for WiFi routers, strong demand for low 
Earth orbit (LEO) satellite communications, and a continued ramp in pin 
diode (PD) sales after the product entered mass-production from late-
2Q26. This should be followed by laser diode (LD), such as continuous 
wave (CW) and electro-absorption modulated laser (EML), though 
customer qualifications will take time. All the commentary reinforces our 
thesis that 3 growth pillars will help turn around WinSemi’s bottom line for 
profitability optimisation after its business transformation: 1) RF business 
transformation paying off with premiumisation across cellular and WiFi for 
differentiation, 2) high-margin infrastructure scaling up on a robust LEO 
build, and 3) AI OC ramping fast, with broadened offerings from peripheral 
chips (optical drivers and modulators) to core chips for PD and LD.

What we recommend: Given our forecast revisions, we lift our 12M TP for 
WinSemi to TWD620 (previous: TWD449), based on a higher 4-quarter 
forward PER target of 55x (previous: 50x), justified by its 2025-28E EPS 
CAGR of 54% (previous: 50%) from a PEG perspective. Yet we lower our 
rating by one notch to Outperform (2) on valuation grounds, with key 
downside risk of commodity cost hikes worsening consumer demand.

How we differ: Our 2026E EPS is 40% above consensus due to our 
inclusion of the non-operating gains in 3Q26, while our 2027E EPS is 9% 
above likely on our higher margin assumptions.

29 October 2026

Downgrading: profit optimisation on the go

➢ Raising our forecasts on a higher margin trajectory 
➢ Growth pillars paying off across infrastructure, optics and RF 
➢ Downgrading our rating to Outperform (2), with a higher TP of TWD620

Company profile 
Founded in 1999, Win Semiconductors Corp (WinSemi) is the world’s largest compound semiconductor 
foundry, focusing on gallium-arsenide (GaAs) foundry services for customers in both wireless and wire-
line communication markets, as well as infrastructure applications. It has a diverse technology portfolio 
of processes that supports microwave frequency requirements from 50MHz to 100GHz, with 
applications including smartphones, tablet PCs, cellular base-stations, very small aperture terminals 
(VSAT) and fibre optics. It has started penetrating into vertical cavity surface emitting laser (VCSEL) 
foundries since 2017 for consumer laser applications such as 3D laser sensors and cameras.

### Page 5

5

Win Semiconductors (3105 TT): 29 October 2026 
ESG analysis

Update Date: 4 May 2026 
Source: Daiwa, Company

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 2
WinSemi's board of directors (BoD) has 10 members, including 3 independent directors,
representing 30% of total directors. Among the BoD, three of its non-independent directors are
also executive management.
Capital management 2
Since 2017, its dividend payout ratio has remained above 58%, except for 2024 when no
dividend was paid. Despite being the largest compound semiconductor foundry in the world,
WinSemi is continually expanding its capacity for future growth. Thus, we see the balance
between investment and dividend as appropriate.
Related party &
transaction 1 It did not have any meaningful related-party transactions in 2023.
S Supply chainmanagement 2
WinSemi We actively cooperate with local suppliers and improve their development capabilities,
reduce carbon emissions and create local employment opportunities. WinSemi also conducts on-
site audits of suppliers and annual assessment of suppliers on environmental protection and
safety & health management systems. In 2023, 100% of WinSemi's new suppliers signed the
"WIN Supplier Code of Conduct".'"
E Water & wastewatermanagement 1
Effluent that flows out of WinSemi’s facilities will first undergo pre-treatment in-house and then
treatment by the wastewater treatment plant at Hwaya Technology Park before discharge. The
plant has an acid and alkali wastewater recycling system and chemical mechanical polishing
wastewater recycling system for wastewater management.
E
Waste & hazardous
materials
management
1
WinSemi has established a hazardous substance-free management procedure and also
collaborates with its suppliers to produce products that are hazardous substance-free for
customers and consumers. WinSemi specifies in purchase contracts and order forms with its
suppliers that the provided materials may not contain hazardous substances as defined by
WinSemi.
For waste management, it aims to increase the recycling rate of waste solvents and reduce the
use of raw materials in production.
E GHG emissions 1
For reduction of energy consumption, it prioritises high-efficiency and low energy consumption
models when replacing equipment and has also established annual energy conservation
management objectives. WinSemi targets to reduce electricity consumption (kWh/6"e wafer) by
12% from 2018 to 458.58 and PFCs emission (ton CO2e/6"e wafer) by 50% from 2018 to 0.114
in 2030.
Note: Management score represents a company's ability to manage/benefit from certain ESG topics. The scores range from 1 to 3, with 1 being the strongest.

### Page 1

3-month preview: Landmark’s Sep revenues were up 190% YoY/ 4% MoM to 
NT$540m, supported by strong demand on 800G / 1.6T SiPh optical modules and 
better InP substrate supply. Looking ahead, we expect company’s Oct/ Nov/ Dec 
revenues to see sequential MoM growth at 5%/ 5%/ 8%, resulting in 4Q revenues 
growth at 16% QoQ to NT$1.8bn, with company’s continuous capacity expansion.

### Page 2

Earnings revision: We factor in Landmark 3Q26 result and revise up earnings by 9%/ 
3%/ 2%/ 1% in 2026-29E mainly on higher revenues of SiPh CW lasers, supported by 
strong client demand and capacity expansion. We revise up GM by 1ppts in 2026E on 
mix upgrade, and revise down Opex ratio in 2026-29E to reflect improving operational 
efficiency amid the larger scale.

Valuation: We employ a discounted P/E methodology (unchanged) and apply a 61.5x 
(unchanged, report link) target P/E multiple on our 2029E EPS, discounted back to 
2027E at a COE of 10.5% (beta 1.7x, risk free rate 1.6%, and market risk premium at 
5.1% - unchanged). Our target P/E multiple of 61.5x is derived from the PEG&M ratio of 
peers (unchanged) in the photonics supply chain. Consequently, our 12-month target 
price is now NT$4,490 (vs. previously at NT$4,459). Our new TP implied 2028E P/E of 
75x is between company’s avg. P/E at 62x and avg. +1 P/E at 91x. Maintain Buy.

### Page 3

Price Target Risks and Methodology - LandMark 
Price Target Risks and Methodology - LandMark Optoelectronics Corp.  
Valuation: We employ a discounted P/E methodology, applying a 61.5x target P/E 
multiple on our 2029E EPS, then discount it back to 2027E at a COE of 10.5% (beta 1.7x, 
risk free rate 1.6% and market risk premium at 5.1%), to derive our 12-month target 
price of NT$4,490. Our target P/E multiple is based on the same PEG&M ratio as peers in 
the photonics supply chain. We are Buy-rated on LandMark. 
Key risk: Slower-than-expected SiPh penetration; stronger-than-expected competition; 
Integrated Device Manufacturers (IDMs) offering Epitaxial (EPI) wafers in-house.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.
Coverage Universe: A list of all stocks in each coverage universe is available by primary analyst, stock and coverage universe at

https://www.gs.com/research/hedge.html.

Goldman Sachs is acting in an advisory capacity in a merger or in a strategic transaction involving this company, when there are legal, regulatory or
policy constraints due to Goldman Sachs’ involvement in a transaction, and in certain other circumstances. Early-Stage Biotech (ES). An investment

rating and a target price are not assigned pursuant to Goldman Sachs policy when this company has neither a drug, treatment or medical device that
has passed a Phase II clinical trial nor a license to distribute a post-Phase II drug, treatment or medical device.  Rating Suspended (RS). Goldman Sachs

Greater China Technology Hardware
Asia Pacific
Industry View In-Line
In this report, we focus on China's manufacturing PMI, which we
see as a potential catalyst for AirTAC's and Hiwin's share prices.
China's manufacturing PMI fell to 49.3 in October from 50.1 in September:

• This came in lower than both our estimate and consensus of 49.9.
• The production index decreased 1.9ppt while the new order index declined
1.1ppt MoM.
Our view:
• We believe the soft manufacturing PMI data could weigh on market
sentiment in the near term. Nonetheless, we prefer supply players with
company-specific drivers such as Airtac and Hiwin:
° Airtac (1590.TW, OW) should continue to benefit from its own share
gains and incremental contribution from linear guides. Valuation appears

undemanding at 16x 2027e P/E, vs. the 25x average since 2020 (see our
30 Oct 2026 report).
° Hiwin (2049.TW, OW) should show more margin expansion in coming
quarters, supported by higher utilization and price hikes. We see
potential share price upside at the current 28x 2027e P/E, vs. peak cycle
valuation of 35-40x.
Morgan Stanley does and seeks to do business with
companies covered in Morgan Stanley Research. As a result,
investors should be aware that the firm may have a conflict of

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.

Morgan Stanley Research 3
Valuation Methodology and Risks
AirTAC International (1590.TW)
Base case, 29x 2027e P/E. We believe this methodology better captures earnings growth
momentum for industrial automation component plays (market sentiment moves along
with business cycles). As the industry enters an up-cycle, we think applying a near-peak cycle
valuation of 29x P/E is reasonable given the stronger business outlook.
Risks to Upside
n Stronger macroeconomic climate in China

n Stronger-than-expected demand from industrial automation
Risks to Downside
n Sharper economic downturn in China
n Miniature linear guideway business development taking more time
n Larger-than-expected investment in new products but weaker-than-expected demand
Hiwin Technologies Corp. (2049.TW)
Base case, P/E. We think this valuation methodology better captures Hiwin's earnings growth
momentum, as its valuation tends to move with the business cycle. We apply a target multiple

of 38x to our 2027 EPS estimate. We view this multiple as justified by the 47% operating profit
CAGR we project for 2025-28.
Risks to Upside
n Stronger industrial automation demand pickup.
n Market share gains.
n Earlier-than-expected revenue contribution from humanoids.
Risks to Downside
n Weaker industrial automation demand.
n More pricing pressure.
n Market share loss.
n More broad-based impact on the global economy from geopolitical risks.

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

### Page 1

Taiwan Industrials

What’s new: We provide a 3Q26 results preview on Sunon, which plans to 
release its 3Q26 results on 6 November. We reaffirm our Buy (1) call as we 
expect the 4Q26 and 2027 outlook for its telecom, servers, industrial and 
auto segments to remain intact with both gross and operating margin set to 
expand. We now forecast a net profit CAGR of 28.2% over 2026-28E from 
21.2% previously (see our flash, 28 August).

What’s the impact: 3Q26 results preview. Sunon’s 3Q26 revenue of 
TWD6,143m (+13.4% QoQ and +24.5% YoY), a record-high level, 
accounted for 97.2% and 102.2%, respectively, of our previous 
(TWD6,319m, +16.6% QoQ and +28.1% YoY) and the Bloomberg 
consensus estimates for 3Q26 revenue. We forecast its 3Q26 gross margin 
to be 32.8% (vs. 31.3% in 2Q26 and 31.3% in 3Q25), slightly higher than 
the Bloomberg consensus estimate of 31.9%. We forecast an operating 
profit of TWD1,115m (+33.3% QoQ and +45.0% YoY), 3.0% higher than 
the Bloomberg consensus estimate of TWD1,082m and an operating 
margin of 18.1% (vs. 15.4% in 2Q26 and 15.6% in 3Q25). Overall, we 
forecast a net profit of TWD901m (+30.5% QoQ, +29.7% YoY), 1.9% 
higher than the consensus estimate of TWD884m. We forecast 4Q26 
revenue to grow 7.5% QoQ, and 1Q27 revenue to grow 1.4% QoQ 
considering its ASIC and GPU projects.

Rising liquid cooling revenue and EC fan exposure in 2027E underpin 
its gross margin expansion. We expect Sunon’s cold plate products (ie, 
enterprise and GPU clients), pump and CDU to be the key revenue drivers 
with certification also underway for CSP clients. We expect revenue from 
EC fans to reach 10% in 2027E for the industrial segment. We revise up 
our 2026-28E EPS by 1-13%, mainly on higher revenue, gross margin and 
operating margin assumptions.

What we recommend: We reaffirm our Buy (1) call and raise our 12M TP 
to TWD265 (from TWD236), still based on a target PER of 18x vs. its past-
5-year PER range of 6-25x, on the 2027E EPS, given solid earnings 
visibility from intact demand in the server, telecom, industrial and auto 
segments. We believe its dividend yield of 5.6-9.2% over 2026-28E will 
offer downside support for its share price. Downside risks: worse-than-
expected gross-margin expansion and AI server development progress.

How we differ: We are one of only two overseas brokers covering the 
stock. Our 2026-28E EPS are 4-12% above the Bloomberg consensus, 
likely due to our more aggressive revenue, gross margin and operating 
margin estimates.

Company profile 
Founded in 1980 and listed on Taiwan’s main board in 2000, Sunonwealth Electric Machine Industry 
(Sunon) is one of the top-4 fan and motor providers in the world, along with Delta Electronics, Nidec and 
ebm-papst. Its major clients are Apple, Dell, Lenovo, Bosch, Siemens and Tesla. Its applications include 
IT & OE, telecoms & servers, industrial & medical equipment, home applications, distribution, autos and 
LED lighting.

### Page 5

5

Sunonwealth Electric Machine Industry (2421 TT): 3 November 2026 
ESG analysis

Update Date: 8 May 2026 
Source: Daiwa, Company

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 2
The CEO and chairperson of Sunonwealth are the same individual; Sunonwealth's board is led
by the Hung family. We see no issues with the company’s board. We view the chairman leading
the R&D team as a sign of the company’s continuing focus on developing new products to
achieve a long-term competitive advantage.
Capital management 2 Sunonwealth's dividend payout ratio was 83-88% in 2015-18, but it decreased to 60-69% during2022-25 due to its capacity expansion plans.
Related party &
transaction 2 Revenue generated from related-party transactions was less than 1% of total revenue during2023-25.
E Materials sourcing &efficiency 1
Sunonwealth’s sourcing of raw materials has been relatively stable (including IC, copper, plastic,
aluminium and steel), and we also note that Sunonwealth abides by RoHS regulations and
relevant international standards (ISO14001, IECQ QC080000, ISO9001) for its material sourcing
activities. With the ongoing ESG trend, its clients may move towards a more energy-efficient
production cycle, which will likely benefit Sunonwealth’s energy-efficient products.
E Product design &lifecycle management 2
Sunonwealth has improved its product designs to stronger but lighter form factors. Besides, we
have observed more green projects utilising Sunonwealth's capabilities. Thus, we believe
Sunonwealth has strong product design and lifecycle management capabilities to address the
ongoing changing business environment.
S Product quality &safety 2 Sunonwealth has its own Quality Strategy Centre to evaluate quality issues. With internationalstandards in place, we believe Sunonwealth has taken relevant measures to address productquality and safety issues.
Note: Management score represents a company's ability to manage/benefit from certain ESG topics. The scores range from 1 to 3, with 1 being the strongest.

Document 191: 35岁不会写代码？这波 AI 红利，可能就轮到你了

Source PDF: all report pdf/35岁不会写代码？这波 AI 红利，可能就轮到你了.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-05
Pages: 8
Status: ok

### Page 1

最缺的不是更强的  AI 模型，⽽是既懂业务、⼜能把  AI 真正⽤起来的⼈。
摘要：
今年以来，全球科技公司已宣布裁员近  20 万⼈。与此同时，有⼀个  AI 岗位的招聘需求却在爆
发：美国的招聘⼴告三年涨了  30 多倍，薪资中位数约  19.5 万美元；国内相关职位的发布量⼀
年涨了  21 倍。
OpenAI 、 Anthropic 、微软、亚⻢逊、⾕歌，⼏家巨头前后砸下近百亿美元，有的开新公司，有
的建新部⻔，抢的都是这类⼈才。最反常识的是：这个岗位最稀缺的本事，恰恰不是写代码。
Anthropic ：培养  1 万个 “ 把  AI 送进企业的⼈ ”
10 ⽉  2 ⽇， Claude 的开发商  Anthropic 宣布投⼊  1 亿美元成⽴ “Claude 前沿学院 ” （ Claude
Frontier Academy ），⽬标是在  2027 年底前培养  1 万名 “ 前沿部署⼯程师 ” 。
⾸批学员是从合作伙伴中选出的在职⼯程师，来⾃埃森哲、摩根⼠丹利、诺和诺德和⼏家顶级
咨询公司。培训分两段：先是  4 天线下集训，从⼀次 “ 模拟企业部署 ” 开始；考核通过后，学员回
到⾃⼰公司，带⼀个真实项⽬做  12 周实战。⾸批认证预计在  2027 年初完成。
在  Anthropic 看来，企业落地  AI 的瓶颈不在模型，⽽在于缺少能推动变⾰的⼈。负责这个项⽬
的⾼管  Steve Corfield 说，客⼾现在缺的，是既懂业务怎么运转、⼜能亲⼿把  AI 从⼀个想法做
成上线系统的⼈。
这只是今年  AI 巨头抢⼈⼤战中的最新⼀例。

砸下百亿美元，巨头们都在抢同⼀种⼈才
今年  5 ⽉， OpenAI 成⽴了专⻔帮企业落地  AI 的 “OpenAI 部署公司 ” ，由  TPG 领投，⾸轮融资
超过  40 亿美元。同⽉， Anthropic 与⿊⽯、 Hellman & Friedman 、⾼盛合资成⽴了⼀家  AI 服
务公司，各⽅初始投资约  15 亿美元。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

6 ⽉底到  7 ⽉初，亚⻢逊  AWS 和微软先后出⼿： AWS 投⼊  10 亿美元，组建⾃⼰的部署⼯程
团队；微软投⼊  25 亿美元成⽴新部⻔，把约  6000 名⼯程专家派驻到客⼾企业。 9 ⽉，⾕歌云
和埃森哲成⽴联合业务部⻔，计划培训最多  1000 名埃森哲⼯程师，专⻔派驻客⼾现场。
过去这些公司⽐的是谁的模型更强、参数更⼤、跑分更⾼，如今却不约⽽同地把钱砸向同⼀个
⽅向：派⼈进客⼾公司，把  AI 真正⽤起来。
这类⼈才在业内有个统⼀的叫法：FDE （ Forward Deployed Engineer ），中⽂常译作“ 前沿部
署⼯程师 ”。
招聘量三年猛涨 30 倍，⼤⼚开出百万年包
熟悉美股的读者，对这个词的源头不会陌⽣。⼗多年前， Palantir （帕兰泰尔）就系统化地推⾏
了这种做法：⼯程师不坐在总部写通⽤产品，⽽是常驻客⼾现场，和客⼾⽤同⼀套数据、扛同
⼀个业务指标，把模糊的需求、分散的数据⼀点点变成能⽤的系统。 Palantir CEO Alex Karp 有

⼀个被业内反复引⽤的判断：⼤模型当然重要，但⾄少在未来⼏年，价值在于落地实施。
如今，这套做法正在变成⼀个热⻔职业。据劳动⼒市场分析机构  Lightcast 统计， 2023 年全美
FDE 招聘⼴告不到  200 条， 2026 年前九个⽉已超过  7300 条，三年涨了  30 多倍。⼴告标出的
薪资中位数约  19.5 万美元，是全美招聘⼴告薪资中位数的三倍多。发布  FDE ⼴告最多的雇主
中，德勤、埃森哲等咨询公司排在⾕歌等科技巨头之前。可⻅  FDE 早已不只是  AI 公司的技术
岗，凡是要把  AI ⽤进业务的地⽅，都需要这样的⼈。
FDE 这股⻛已经刮到国内。脉脉平台数据显⽰， 2026 年上半年，平台上  FDE 相关职位的发布
量同⽐飙升  21 倍。字节跳动的 “ ⾖包  AI ⼤模型  FDE” 年包最⾼约  105 万元，蚂蚁数科、智谱等
也开出了  90 万元以上的年包。但即便开出⾼薪，合适的⼈依然难招：猎聘⼤数据研究院统计，
2025 年  9 ⽉⾄  2026 年  8 ⽉，国内  FDE ⼈才的供需⽐只有  0.28 ，相当于将近  4 个岗位抢  1 个

⼈。
这个缺⼝还在被政策放⼤。⼯信部  8 ⽉底印发通知，⿎励  AI 应⽤服务商搭建  FDE 团队、扎根
⽤⼾现场；武汉光⾕提出  3 年培育  1000 名  FDE ，上海提出要建⽴超千⼈规模的  FDE ⼯程师储
备库。企业、服务商、地⽅政府都在要⼈，合格的  FDE 远远不够⽤。
AI 落地难在业务，最缺的恰恰是懂业务的⼈
看到这⾥，很多⼈会想：这是年轻程序员的机会，我不写代码，也过了转⾏的年纪。但不妨看
看  Anthropic 怎么描述⼀个好的  FDE ：熟悉  Agent 系统、擅⻓解决问题、善于沟通、有⼯程经
验。这四条⾥，有⼀半在技术之外。
AI 落地最难的部分，本来就不在代码。过去两年，⼏乎每家企业都买过  AI 产品、做过试点，真
正跑进⽇常业务的却少得多。标普全球的调查显⽰，放弃⼤部分  AI 项⽬的企业，⼀年内从  17%
涨到了  42% ； IDC 和联想  2025 年的报告更直接： 88% 的  AI 概念验证项⽬，最后都没能投⼊
⽣产。
演⽰时⼀切顺利，⼀进真实业务，就撞上脏数据、⽼系统、权限审批、员⼯习惯，还有那个绕

不开的问题：出了错谁负责。这也是  FDE 和驻场外包的区别：外包是甲⽅说什么就做什么；
FDE 要先判断这件事该不该⽤  AI 、⽤哪个模型、花多少钱、怎么证明有效，再动⼿上线。
所以  FDE 真正值钱的那⼀半能⼒，是懂业务。⼀份合同哪⼏条要紧，⼀张报表哪个数字会出
事，⼀个审批流程卡在哪⼀步，哪件事交给机器会让⽼板睡不着觉，这些  AI 替代不了，在⾏业
⾥⼲了⼗⼏、⼆⼗年的⼈反⽽最清楚。

### Page 3

腾讯云副总裁张渝前不久的⼀句话，戳中了很多企业的痛处：过去花⼤价钱请外部团队做项
⽬， " 项⽬做完，团队撤场，能⼒也跟着带⾛了 " 。越来越多企业意识到，与其等外部团队来了⼜
⾛，不如让⾃⼰懂业务的⼈⻓出这项能⼒。
如果你已经在⾏业⾥深耕多年，那么你缺的从来不是经验，⽽是把经验交给  AI 去执⾏的那⼀
步：知道今天的  AI 能做什么、做不了什么，该怎么搭、要花多少钱、出了错怎么兜底。我们这
次的训练营，就是为这⼀步准备的。
两天⾛完  AI 落地最后⼀公⾥
华尔街⻅闻⾸期「 FDE 实战训练营」将于  11 ⽉  14 ⽇⾄  15 ⽇在上海开班。这是⼀个线下实战
营，限量  50 席，在两天时间⾥，带你亲⼿搭出⾃⼰的第⼀个  AI Agent ，⾛完⼀遍从选模型、算
成本到评测上线的全流程。
先说说  Agent （智能体）是什么。很多⼈对  AI 的印象还停留在 “ 问⼀句答⼀句 ” 的聊天上，但
Agent 不⼀样：你交给它⼀件事，它会⾃⼰查资料、打开软件、读写⽂件、交出结果，做错了还
能⾃⼰返⼯。巨头们派  FDE 进企业，部署的正是这种技术。
两天训练营共  12 个模块，从模型怎么选、价格怎么算，⼀直讲到评测、成本优化和安全上线，
都是⼤⼚把  AI 做上线时真正在⽤的⽅法。主讲讲师  Right 曾任腾讯⾼级算法⼯程师，主导过微
信核⼼  AI Agent 项⽬的算法设计和规模化落地，在  Agent 系统设计、模型训练、 Harness 架构
⽅向有深厚积累。他还在  NeurIPS 、 ACL 等顶级会议发表过多篇  Agent ⽅向论⽂，相关⼯作被
Google DeepMind 引⽤。
课程适合谁
企业主和管理者：想弄清  AI 到底能替公司做哪些事、每件事花多少钱、⻛险在哪⾥
⾦融、法务、财务、运营、咨询等专业⼈⼠：⼿上有⼤量重复⼯作，想亲⼿做⼀个⼯具交给
AI
想在  AI 时代给⾃⼰多留⼀项硬技能的⼈

三⼗年前，先学会⽤  Excel 的那批⼈，在后来的⼆⼗年⾥少熬了⽆数个夜。
今天巨头们花百亿美元在抢懂业务、⼜会⽤  AI 的⼈，⽽你已经有了⼀半。剩下那⼀半，就在这
两天⾥补⻬。

### Page 1

2026 年 10 月 30 日(金)

1/5 
AI バブル測定器としての鉱工業生産（26 年９月） 
 経済調査部主席エコノミスト藤代宏一 
（℡：050-5474-6123）

・筆者が日本経済と株式市場の行方を読む上で定点観測している日本の鉱工業生産は、2026年
９月に前月比＋2.2％となり、市場予想（同＋1.3％）を上回った。経産産業省の予測補正値
（同＋1.6％）に対しても上振れた。増産は３ヶ月ぶり。８月が速報段階の同▲1.7％から確
報で同▲2.4％へと下方修正されていたため、９月の増産で前月までの落ち込みを概ね取り戻
した格好となる。もっとも、生産水準は直近最高だった６月の104.6を上回るには至っておら
ず、数量ベースの生産活動は、経済産業省の判断通り「一進一退」となっている。

・日経平均株価は先行き12 ヶ月72,000円程度で推移するだろう 
・USD/JPYは先行き12ヶ月 155円程度で推移するだろう 
・日銀は政策金利を12月に 1.5％とした後、27年末までに2.25％とするだろう 
・FEDはFF金利を、年内は4.0％で据え置くだろう

### Page 2

2/5 
・増産となったのは８業種。上位３業種は自動車工業、生産用機械工業、汎用・業務機械工業
であった。反対に減産の寄与度が大きかった３業種は鉄鋼・非鉄金属、窯業・土石製品工
業、化学工業（除く無機・有機化学工業・医薬品）であった。７月に熊本で発生した地震に
よって８月までは自動車生産などに影響が出ていたが、その挽回生産が９月の数値を押し上
げる格好となった。

・10月初旬に実施された生産予測調査に基づけば、製造工業の生産計画は10月に前月比＋5.1％
となった後、11月は同＋0.8％と増産が見込まれている。経産省経済解析室が生産予測指数の
上振れバイアスを補正した10月の予測値は同＋3.9％とかなり強い。電子部品・デバイス工業
と生産用機械工業で大幅な増産が見込まれているほか、自動車の挽回生産も続く見込みであ
る。生産活動全体の方向感を決める輸送機械工業は９月（実績）に前月比＋5.5％となった
後、10月（計画）に同＋0.9％、11月に同＋4.3％となっている。日本や米国における自動車
販売台数が堅調に推移していることを踏まえると、基調的に弱含んでいく展開は想定しにく
い。また生産活動の先行指標として有用な製造業ＰＭＩが４月以降に有意な上昇を示してい
ることから判断すると、製造業全体としてはＡＩ・半導体需要が浸透する形で増勢が強まっ
ていくと期待される。10月製造業ＰＭＩは53.4と高水準を維持している。

・なお、鉱工業生産指数は生産の「数量」に重きを置いて生産高を計測する統計であり、「付
加価値」を直接捕捉する性格のものではない。例えば、高付加価値を狙った品質向上によっ
て生産数量・重量が減少する場合、統計上はそれを減産と見做し、付加価値を過小評価して
しまうこともある。したがって、鉱工業生産統計が示すほど日本経済（製造業）の付加価値

3/5 
創出力が停滞していない可能性を念頭に置く必要がある。事実、過去数年は付加価値の合計
である実質ＧＤＰと鉱工業生産指数の水準・方向感の乖離が大きくなっている。

・株式市場における重要度が高い電子部品・デバイス工業については、前月比＋3.6％の増産で
あった。地震の影響もあってか、ＩＣの増産に一服感がみられる一方、コンデンサや電子回
路基板などで増産の動きがみられる。生産用機械工業に分類される半導体製造装置は同＋
3.4％の増産となり、３ヶ月平均値でみても＋7.7％と力強い増産基調にある。生産予測調査
に目を向けると、電子部品・デバイス工業は10月に同＋16.6％、11月に同▲1.7％と均してみ
れば大幅な増産が計画されている。半導体製造装置が含まれる生産用機械工業は10月に同＋
15.5％と大幅増産となった後、11月は同▲5.7％と減産が見込まれているが、こちらも基調は
強い。個別企業の業績予想などから判断しても、先行きは半導体製造装置の増産がかなりの
高確度で期待できる。関連指標の機械受注統計における「電子計算機等」の受注額水準も鈍
化する気配に乏しい。この指標は日経平均株価との連動性がある。

4/5

・電子部品・デバイス工業の動向を仔細にみると、９月は出荷が前年比＋0.8％へと減速するな
か、在庫は同▲6.1％へとマイナス幅を拡大した。この結果、出荷・在庫バランス（両者の前
年比差分から算出）は＋6.9pとなり、３ヶ月平均でも＋7.0ptとはっきりとしたプラス領域を
維持した。引き続き、製品需給が引き締まる方向にあることが示唆されている。ここで電子
部品・デバイス工業の出荷・在庫バランスと、日経平均株価が長期的に連動性を有してきた
事実を再確認したい。株価指数において半導体を直接手掛ける企業の存在感は必ずしも大き
くはないものの、半導体製造装置、電子部品、化学品など「広義半導体」で見ればその存在
感は大きく、結果的に日本株全体のうねりを作り出す構図があると筆者は理解している。ち
なみに出荷在庫バランス株価は方向感こそ一致しているものの、両者のかい離は大きくなっ
ている。その要因として、数量ベースの出荷・在庫バランスには、メモリなど製品価格の高
騰が反映されないことが考えられる。

### Page 5

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限

Document 321: Cliff Notes_ getting the balance right (2026-11-05)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Cliff Notes_ getting the balance right (2026-11-05).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-05
Pages: 10
Status: ok

### Page 1

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: getting the balance right
By Ryan Wells, Illiana Jain & Elliot Clarke
10:46 October 16 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
Australia’s key data release this week was the September Labour Force Survey. Employment surprised
to the downside, -39.3k. However, a 0.2ppt decline in labour force participation from August’s near-
historic high largely offset. The unemployment rate therefore held at 4.7% after August was revised up.

Residual seasonality may be in the mix. Regardless, the take home point from the update is that the

### Page 2

labour market is continuing to ease as employment growth runs below working-age population growth.
We expect the unemployment rate to grind higher into 2027 as tighter financial conditions and cost-of-
living pressures restrict activity growth to a below-trend pace. Although, our forecast peak is near at
4.8%, due by Q2 2027.
This update on the labour market complicates the communication challenge for the RBA. The
September minutes were firmly on the hawkish side, with the discussion focussing on materialising

upside risks to inflation. But, with the unemployment rate now through the RBA’s NAIRU assumption of
4.6%, Governor Bullock’s “a little tight” descriptor for the labour market is becoming harder to defend.
Still, with a clear focus on inflation risks – which also led us to revise up our near-term CPI forecasts in
the October Market Outlook – we still expect a 25bp rate hike at the November meeting.
Over in the US, the headline CPI rose 0.6% as expected in September, taking the annual rate up to

3.7%yr. Energy prices were a key contributor (5.2%, 20.4%yr), while core inflation was a touch above
consensus in the month at 0.3% (2.6%yr). Within the core components, core goods prices gained 0.3%
after being broadly unchanged over the past 6 months (0.7%yr). Core services meanwhile rose 0.4%
(3.1%yr) on a 0.4% increase in shelter (3.2%yr), a 0.6% gain for transport services and a 0.3%
increase in medical care services (2.7%yr and 2.5%yr respectively). Further, the subsequent release of

the PPI pointed to continued upstream price pressures, with the headline and core measures up
6.3%yr and 5.0%yr respectively. The latest Beige Book provided colour on these developments, firms
across multiple districts citing tariffs, insurance and commodity prices as ongoing sources of cost
pressure. Guiding on the risks, many respondents expect further price increases as contracts/hedges
roll off.
Given their telegraphed concerns over inflation, there is enough signal in these reports to warrant a

follow-up hike at the October FOMC meeting. Though it seems Committee members believe they are
near the end of the tightening cycle and so have time on their side. This week, Daly and Bowman both
showed patience in assessing the trend, with no clear bias towards an October or December decision.
Williams stated he believes one more hike would prove sufficient in this cycle given known information.
And Waller also showed confidence, highlighting that elevated long-term yields are doing some of the

FOMC’s work, restricting demand in interest rate sensitive sectors. We hold a similar view, anticipating
that one more hike by year end and a little time will bring inflation near the 2.0%yr medium-term target.
However, as discussed in this month’s Market Outlook, capacity pressures are likely to see inflation
consistently above target, rather than below, in coming years – particularly if, as we suspect, capacity
in the housing sector remains tight. This will mean the FOMC have to remain modestly restrictive over

the forecast horizon and will also bias up long-term yields.
In Asia, there was one release that stood out, China’s trade account. At USD133.5bn, the September
result was a whisker inside January 2025’s record high of USD137.9bn. More impressively, throughout
2025 and 2026 year-to-date the surplus has now averaged USD100.9bn, almost 3 times the average of
2019, prior to the pandemic. The geographic breadth of export growth is as impressive as the headline

annual gain of 36.3%yr, even more so given the ongoing strength in Chinese investment in
neighbouring nations’ production chains. In time, both trade and income receipts from offshore
investment should buoy the renminbi, although for now continued outbound foreign investment should
moderate the pace of gains against other nations in the region, and likely versus the US dollar.

### Page 3

Related articles
Another key trend assessed in October’s Market Outlook is the evolution of producer prices across
Asia, and the likelihood of the disinflationary benefit the west has received in recent decades
continuing. Historically, labour cost arbitrage was one of the key drivers of declining goods prices.
While still possible, we expect the next phase of manufacturing development to instead be driven by
capital, technology and energy. Many end users and the manufacturers undertaking production are

also increasingly prioritising resilience, security and specialisation over cost minimisation. It is therefore
more probably future supply-chain developments will help contain rising costs through productivity than
drive sustained price declines for the goods bought by consumers and businesses.

Document 356: First Impressions_ NZ labour market surveys, September quarter 2026 (2026-11-05)

Source PDF: all report pdf/First Impressions_ NZ labour market surveys, September quarter 2026 (2026-11-05).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-05
Pages: 10
Status: ok

### Page 1

#labour #newzealand
First Impressions: NZ labour market surveys, September quarter
2026
By Michael Gordon
Senior Economist, Westpac NZ
09:01 November 04 2026 Share
The unemployment rate rose to 5.7% in the September quarter, against expectations for a
small decline. Spare capacity in the labour market is keeping wage pressures subdued.
Unemployment rate: 5.7% (prev: 5.6%, Westpac: 5.5%, RBNZ: 5.5%, mkt: 5.5%)
Employment change: -0.1% (prev: +0.3%, Westpac: +0.5%, RBNZ: +0.3%, mkt: +0.4%)

### Page 2

Participation rate: 70.4% (prev: 70.6%, Westpac: 70.8%, RBNZ: 70.6%, mkt: 70.7%) 
Labour costs (private sector): +0.5% (prev: +0.7%, Westpac: +0.5%, RBNZ: +0.4%, mkt: +0.5%)

The September quarter surveys pointed to ongoing softness in New Zealand’s labour market. The
unemployment rate rose from 5.6% to 5.7%, continuing an almost five-year run of increases.
Employment was softer than expected, and wage growth remains subdued despite increasing cost-of-
living pressures. 
The Household Labour Force Survey (HLFS) showed a surprise 0.1% fall in employment in the
September quarter, after a downwardly revised 0.3% increase in the June quarter. That went against
the Monthly Employment Indicator (MEI) measure of filled jobs, which had suggested some upward
momentum emerging in the jobs market (and notably, the HLFS measure had outpaced the MEI for the
previous nine quarters). It was also softer than suggested by the business-focused Quarterly
Employment Survey (QES), which recorded a 0.2% rise in full-time equivalent employees, a 0.1% rise
in filled jobs and a 0.6% rise in hours paid. 
The fall in HLFS employment was accompanied by a fall in labour force participation rate, from 70.6%
to 70.4%. These co-movements are effectively a reversal of the surprisingly large increases that we
saw in the June quarter, which we noted at the time were indicative of sampling error in the survey. 
This is why we generally recommend focusing on the unemployment rate, as it’s less vulnerable to
sampling error. And in that respect, the message on the jobs market remains weak: to the extent that
employment may be rising, it’s still not fast enough to absorb the growth in the population (which is
itself a fairly low bar, as net migration remains at a relatively low ebb). 
The HLFS also recorded a sharp 1.6% fall in hours worked (although usual hours worked were down
just 0.4%). This seems to have been influenced by Matariki – a floating holiday that was only
introduced a few years ago, so will not be picked up by the seasonal adjustment. This may have also
been responsible for a marked shift from full-time to part-time employment, as some people may have
been pushed into the part-time category simply by virtue of only working four days during the survey
week. 
The Labour Cost Index (LCI) rose by 0.5% in the September quarter, in line with our expectations
overall but a bit weaker in the details – private sector wages were up by 0.5%, while there was a
surprisingly large 0.7% increase in public sector wages. There was a notable jump in the number of
employers citing cost-of-living adjustments as a reason for increasing pay rates, although the size of
those increases varied a great deal, with many substantially less than the headline inflation rate. 
Overall, we think the details are soft from the RBNZ’s perspective – an important consideration ahead
of the 9 December Monetary Policy Statement, as there are few other major data releases between
now and then. The unemployment rate is a more reliable signal than the employment measures, and
the ongoing rise in unemployment points to more slack in the labour market than the RBNZ was hoping
to see in an economy that has been growing for over a year now. The doves on the policy committee
will be encouraged in their view that there is limited scope for headline inflation shocks to pass through
into second-round effects. 
Michael Gordon, Senior Economist
P: +64 21 749 506

Document 357: First Impressions_ RBNZ Monetary Policy Review October 2026 (2026-10-01)

### Page 1

#rbnz #newzealand
First Impressions: RBNZ Monetary Policy Review October 2026
By Kelly Eckhold & Westpac New Zealand Economics
12:30 October 28 2026 Share
The RBNZ left the OCR unchanged at 2.75% (in contrast to market expectation for a hike,
including ourselves).
The MPC has stuck to its strategy of pausing to assess the impact of tightening already done. There is
a clear split among the MPC members on where the risks lie although it seems clear enough that

higher interest rates are in the future the data evolves as the Committee expects. Key factors to focus
on are data on inflation expectations, the global economy (and potential downside risks there) and the

### Page 2

path of fuel prices.  We expect a 25 bp increase in the OCR in December to 3% and are reverting to
the OCR profile we had up until mid last week.  We don't think the December hike is a done deal as the
data will inform the next move. We will revert with more detail after the press conference. 
Key takeouts from the minutes below.

Media contact
Kelly Eckhold
Chief economist 
+64 9 348 9382
+64 21 786 758

The next policy
decision
Future policy decisions will depend
on the Committee’s judgement of the
balance of risks to medium-term
inflation.
Members discussed that information
arriving over the next six weeks
could meaningfully change the
appropriate policy stance in either
direction.
  Conditional on the central economic
outlook, members judged that the
OCR may need to increase further.
All members agreed that tighter
monetary policy is likely to be
required, but remains conditional on
the economic outlook.
  However, the future OCR path is not
pre-determined.

direction for the
OCR
The Committee judges that gradually
removing monetary stimulus is
appropriate to return inflation to the
2 percent target mid-point while
supporting growth and employment.
Further OCR increases likely, but
are conditional on the outlook

moderate sufficiently or broaden
beyond what costs and normal pass-
through can explain, this could
warrant further tightening.

this could alter the desired stance,

easing.

the neutral interest rate has a wide
range of plausible estimates.
    Members noted that the increase in
global long-term yields may partly
signal a higher global neutral
interest rate, due to higher inflation
expectations, war destruction and
demand for capital to support AI
investment.

bring forward a potential global

rate could decline rapidly in the
event of an abrupt global
adjustment.

subdued and higher prices will
further reduce real incomes and
purchasing power.
  Employment growth has not been
sufficient to fully absorb new
entrants into the labour market and
unemployment is elevated,
particularly in Auckland and
Wellington and for youth and the
long-term unemployed.
Employment intentions remain
weak, pointing to continued slow
recovery in the labour market.
  The labour market is expected to
slowly improve, with employment
growing and unemployment falling
as the economy recovers.
To date, wage growth has been
subdued, and medium-term inflation
expectations have remained stable.
  On balance, the Committee
assesses that spare capacity
remains in the economy, particularly
in the labour market.

### Page 6

Category 2 September 2026 meeting 28 October 2026 meeting
The short term
inflation outlook
Inflation increased to 4.1 percent in
the June quarter because of higher
fuel prices arising from the conflict in
the Middle East.
Annual inflation is expected to
increase to 4.4 percent in the
December quarter, largely reflecting
current futures prices for fuel.
  The Committee expects inflation to
remain elevated this year before
returning to the target band by mid-
2027 and reaching the 2 percent
mid-point later next year.
Near-term inflation forecasts are
uncertain given volatility in prices for
oil and refined fuels.
    Direct effects of higher fuel prices
will drop out of the annual inflation
calculation in late 2027 if prices for
refined petroleum products stabilise
or fall.

The medium term
inflation outlook
Core inflation, expected wage
growth, and inflation expectations
remain consistent with inflation
returning to the 1 to 3 percent target
band by mid-2027 and the 2 percent
target midpoint later next year.
The Committee is focused on
returning inflation to the 2 percent
target mid-point over the medium
term, discounting temporary
disturbances to inflation and
avoiding unnecessary economic
instability.
  Longer-term inflation expectations
remain near 2 percent, while most
measures of one and two-year-
ahead inflation expectations have
fallen since May.
Impacts on shortterm pricing
behaviour appear to have been
contained and medium-term inflation
expectations remain well anchored.
  Currently, indicators of medium-term
inflation are consistent with inflation
returning to target.
But the longer inflation remains high,
the greater the risk of higher costs
feeding into inflation expectations
and price- and wage-setting
behaviour, making inflation more
persistent.

### Page 7

Category 2 September 2026 meeting 28 October 2026 meeting

The balance of
risks for
economic growth
and inflation
New Zealand’s economic recovery
could be stronger or weaker than
expected and price pressures could
generate more persistent inflation.
New Zealand’s recovery could be
stronger than expected but could
also lose momentum if the global
environment deteriorates or export
strength does not flow through to
jobs, incomes and household
spending.
  Consumption growth could be
subdued if employment and house
price growth remain weak, or if
precautionary behaviour persists.
There is a risk that inflation becomes
entrenched before conventional
expectations measures respond,
despite the previous two OCR
increases.
  Alternatively, consumption growth
could be stronger and absorb spare
capacity faster than currently
anticipated.
In addition, the world economy could
tip abruptly into a downturn causing
weaker inflation over the medium
term.
  All members agreed that downside
risks to activity were significant and
that the recovery could remain
uneven.
It is also possible that geopolitical
escalation could drive fuel prices
higher while weakening global
economic activity at the same time.

Key arguments
advanced by
more hawkish
MPC members
Hayley Gourley, Karen Silk,
Prasanna Gai and Anna Breman
saw upside risks to inflation relative
to the central projection.
Hayley Gourley, Paul Conway and
Karen Silk favoured increasing the
OCR by 25 basis points at this
meeting.

### Page 8

Category 2 September 2026 meeting 28 October 2026 meeting
  These members observed that more
persistence in energy and
petrochemical prices could raise
near-term inflation risks, impact
price-setting behaviour, and lead to
more sticky inflation over the
medium term.
These members emphasised that
core inflation has been above levels
consistent with headline inflation at
the target mid-point for some time.
  Price-setting can be a front-loaded
process, meaning businesses could
raise domestic prices more than
import costs warrant.
This increases the risk of inflation
becoming entrenched in price-
setting behaviour as the conflict and
its consequences endure, including
through higher freight and shipping
costs.
  These factors increase the risk that
monetary policy needs to lean
against broader inflation pressures.
Acting now would guard against
sustained inflation pressures and
potentially reduce the risk of needing
to do more with interest rates later.

Key arguments
advanced by
more dovish MPC
members in each
meeting
Paul Conway and Carl Hansen saw
risks to inflation as balanced.
Anna Breman, Carl Hansen,
Rebecca Williams and Prasanna Gai
favoured holding the OCR at this
meeting as two recent OCR
increases allow the Committee to
take further time to assess their
effects and economic developments.
  These members shared concerns
that high inflation could become
embedded through price-setting
behaviour.
Actual inflation was lower than
expected, reflecting smaller indirect
effects of higher fuels prices, and
medium-term inflation expectations
and wage growth remain consistent
with returning inflation to the target
mid-point.

Document 431: The Good the Bad and the Ugly for Rates in 2027

Source PDF: all report pdf/The Good the Bad and the Ugly for Rates in 2027.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-11-04
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

The spaghetti-western title neatly captures the three big forces
shaping long-end rates: the ‘good’ of AI-driven productivity, the
‘bad’ of elevated ﬁscal deﬁcits, and the ‘ugly’ of renewed
inﬂationary pressure from ongoing wars. By the middle of 2027,
however, things should start to look a little less ugly as inﬂation
falls

Padhraic Garvey, CFA, Michiel Tukker and Benjamin Schroeder
The good, the bad and the ugly: AI, ﬁscal deﬁcits and inﬂation take centre
stage in the 2027 rates outlook
Key calls and themes and 2027
US rates and a triple whammy of obstacles to overcome
In the US, our baseline view centres on the good, the bad and
the ugly causing trouble in the coming months, and right
through the ﬁrst quarter of 2027 at least, to prompt a return of
the 10yr US Treasury yield to the 5.25%-plus area (vs 4.9%
currently). We’d cap the absolute upside at 5.5%, as waiting
later in 2027 is a 2%-plus handle for GDP growth and inﬂation,
alongside a stable but not worsening debt dynamic narrative.
Cookie settings

### Page 2

The fall in inﬂation here is key, as that allows the Federal
Reserve to step away and for the market to re-discount cuts.
That allows long-dated yields to edge back down, with the
10yr targeting 4.75% for starters and likely settling in the 4.5-
4.75% zone, which is still above normal, but rightfully so. We
don't call for it, but a decision to materially cut long-end
Treasury funding would push in the same direction.
Euro rates have good reason to be nervous

For euro rates, we also expect a bumpy ride at the start of
2027, whereby markets anxiously weigh each inﬂation reading
for signs of second-round eﬀects. If all goes well, we should see
a signiﬁcant repricing of the European Central Bank path,
helping the 2yr EUR swap rate down by some 50bp. The 10yr
rate will be stickier, however, as a global rise in real rates adds
upward pressure to longer euro rates. If inﬂation does not ease
as hoped, then we could see a stretch higher in rates from here.

In such a scenario, the 10yr swap rate could push well above
4%.
European bond spreads in a duel with the "bad"
Fiscal strains and election risk are putting eurozone bonds to
the test. France is the key fault line, while Bunds’ safe-haven
appeal masks mounting supply pressures. We see a turbulent
ﬁrst half of 2027, and any relief hinges on a credible path to
French ﬁscal consolidation
A delayed landing for GBP rates
Sterling rates are well above levels we consider fair value, but

trauma from turning too dovish too quickly is still on investors'
minds. Only once the Bank of England can prove its ability to
bring inﬂation closer to target (by the end of 2027) do we
pencil in a signiﬁcant repricing lower for GBP rates. Relatively
high sensitivity to US rates means we don't expect the path
lower to be a smooth one. And with gilt markets increasingly
reliant on risk-sensitive investors, we think a material term risk
premium remains justiﬁed.

The grand Dutch pension reforms ﬁnale
Dutch pension reforms are reaching their ﬁnale as almost

€900bn of assets are scheduled to transition in January 2027.
The reforms should keep the 10s30s euro swap curve
structurally steeper, but macro will continue to be the main
driver of the curve for now. Under the new system, we expect
more swap ﬂows throughout the year as the need for interest
rate hedges moves in tandem with asset prices (i.e. equities).

Transitioning to mid-cycle 
• We now favour a more balanced stance between stocks and bonds . Near-
term event risks and high  earnings expectations prompt us to trim  equities to a 
Core holding.  The US and Asia ex -Japan (AxJ) remain our preferred regions. 
Within AxJ, we upgrade India to Overweight. 
• Higher bond yields are improving the outlook for future returns and offering 
increasingly attractive  income opportunities. We are Overweight Emerging 
Market (EM) USD bonds and favour corporate over government bonds, but limit 
duration to 3-7 years. 
• We remain  Overweight gold . Prices are likely to grind higher as near -term 
headwinds from higher bond yields are offset by EM central bank demand. 
Great expectations 
Individual risk events, high  earnings expectations and a higher cost of capital 
point to a more moderate return environment ahead.  In the very near term, 
financial markets must navigate key event risks, such as (i) the US mid-term elections, 
which have historically been associated with elevated market volatility and (ii) renewed 
geopolitical tensions, with several reports suggesting the US is considering renewed 
strikes on Iran . These  concerns have pushed the Brent oil price well above USD 
110/bbl despite significant ongoing crude oil flows through the Strait of Hormuz.  
Higher bond yields combined with high earnings expectations raise the risk of 
an expectations reset. The US Q3 earnings season is attracting significant attention 
given high earnings expectations, particularly f or the AI sector. However, higher US 
bond yields may start to bite as debt-financed capex takes on a greater share of total 
capex over the coming year. This, combined with already-high expectations, raises the 
risk of disappointment and a near-term repricing of expected earnings growth pace.  
Despite t he current c hallenges, healthy growth continues to underpin the 
economic backdrop . The US economic outlook remains well supported , and the 
absolute level of year-ahead earnings expectations is high. We do not expect Fed rate 
hikes to result in overly restrictive policy. Our caution, thus, stems from the risk of an 
expectations repricing rather than an outright economic slowdown.

1 Investment strategy and key themes

Steve Brice 
Global Chief Investment Officer 
Manpreet Gill 
Chief Investment Officer, EMEA 
Raymond Cheng, CFA, CPA 
Chief Investment Officer, North Asia

### Page 4

Global Market Outlook 4

Fig. 1 Our quant model score has fallen to levels 
associated with flat-to-modestly-negative equity returns  
 Fig. 2 Negative gold-USD correlation has held back

Our quantitative stock-bond model  Gold and USD correlations over the last 15 years

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered  Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered

A more balanced stock-bond mix 
We trim global equities to a Core holding, leaving us 
Neutral on both equities and bonds and Overweight gold. 
The concerns laid out earlier point to near-term volatility risks 
and a somewhat lower long -term growth trajectory for 
equities. Our quant model also reflects  this cautious outlook, 
having downgraded equities to a level usually associated with 
flat-to-modestly-negative returns. 
From a relative perspective, though, the higher yields on 
offer from bonds also raise the bar  by presenting an 
increasingly compelling return outlook after several 
years of very low yields. We believe this argues for portfolios 
that are increasingly balanced between equities and bonds. 
Ways to diversify equity exposure 
We continue to favour global equity market 
diversification, with a preference for US and AxJ equities. 
We remain Overweight US and A xJ equities, expecting them 
to outperform other major regions. Earnings growth remains 
the main driver of this view – while growth rates could 
decelerate somewhat, we still expect the m to remain higher 
than those of other major regions.  
Japan and Euro area equities remain attractive as 
regional diversifiers.  Our positive view on both markets’  
financial sectors supports our Core holding view on them.   
Within Asia, we are Overweight Taiwan and India.  Indian 
equities have lagged the region this year, in part due to limited 
listed AI -related exposure. However, we believe this is 
increasingly likely to become an advantage as investors seek 
greater diversification away from excessive concentration in 
the AI theme. This comes at a time when the economic and 
earnings growth outlook for Indian equities is starting to 
improve. We retain a more Neutral view on Chinese equities 
amid a still-challenging growth outlook. 
We also favour greater sector diversification . In the US, 
we favour diversifying across our Overweight sectors of 
technology, financials, materials and healthcare. We also 
favour financials in Japan and the Euro area.  
Changes to our equity opportunistic idea s also reflect a 
more defensive stance. In recent weeks, we have closed our 
global semiconductors, MSCI Taiwan, US communication 
services and global power & electrification ideas and have 
opened new ideas on US healthcare and MSCI Switzerland. 
Riding the yield train 
We believe bond markets offer an attractive opportunity 
to lock in yields. The 10-year US government bond yield is 
now in a range last witnessed in 2007. While the process of 
rising yields results in lower bond prices – as painfully 
experienced in recent months  – the yield level itself starts to 
significantly improve the outlook for future returns. 
Favour corporate over government bonds, but check 
your duration. Still-high credit quality means that we continue 
to favour taking on credit risk to earn a higher yield (EM USD 
bonds are preferred, but we also see investment -grade (IG) 
and high -yield (HY) bonds as Core holdings). However, 
limiting duration to 3 -7 years remains key to avoiding 
excessive exposure to any inflation surprises. 
Changes to our bond opportunistic ideas also reflect a 
more balanced stance.  We close our EU  bank contingent 
convertible bonds (CoCos2 – please refer page 29 ) and US 
utilities corporate hybrids ideas. We also close our 
opportunistic idea on US 10-year government bonds following 
the recent pullback in yields, locking in gains. 
Alternatives and gold to remain relevant 
Alternatives are an effective  tool for portfolio risk 
management. Hedge funds and private markets provide a 
differentiated return stream , while  rotating into alternatives  
helps reduce  equity beta exposure . We examine downside 
capture and how beta is reduced via hedge fund sub -
strategies and private markets in our asset allocation section.  
Gold remains a key Overweight. We trim our three- and 12-
month forecasts modestly, but see gold forming a strong base 
above USD 4 ,000/oz. Rising bond yields and the USD were 
recent headwinds, but we expect a softer USD and continued 
EM central bank demand to result in 12-month gains.

2 Foundation: Our tactical asset allocation

### Page 7

Global Market Outlook 7

Summary

Artificial intelligence (AI) has become one of the most powerful drivers of global market performance and, increasingly, 
one of the largest contributors to portfolio risk.  While we remain constructive on the long -term outlook for equities and 
continue to view AI as a transformational investment theme, our tactical positioning has recently shifted from an Overweight to 
a Neutral equity weight. This reflects a more balanced near -term risk -reward profile after a period of strong equity market 
performance and increasingly concentrated market leadership. 
Against this backdrop, a deeper understanding of portfolio construction is essential. Investors should consider not only 
how much they allocate to equities, bonds and alternative investments, but also the underlying factors driving portfolio risk. Using 
a Total Portfolio Approach (TPA), we assessed the factor exposures embedded within our Foundation and Foundation+ 
Balanced portfolios, with a particular focus on AI concentration risk. 
Our analysis suggests both portfolios are more diversified than a traditional 60/40 allocation  and maintain meaningful 
exposure to long -term AI growth opportunities.  The Foundation+ Balanced portfolio provides additional diversification 
through alternative investments and a broader range of return drivers, reducing reliance on public equity markets.

Key findings

Looking beyond asset classes: Understanding 
AI concentration risk 
Investors often assess diversification through the lens of asset 
allocation. A portfolio containing equities, bonds, gold and 
alternatives may appear diversified on the surface. However, 
this traditional approach alone does not always reveal where 
portfolio risks are truly concentrated. 
TPA starts from a different premise. Rather than focusing solely 
on asset classes, it seeks to identify the underlying factors that 
drive portfolio outcomes, including equity markets, interest 
rates, credit spreads, currencies, liquidity conditions and 
thematic exposures such as AI. 
This perspective is particularly relevant following our recent 
move from an Overweight to a Neutral equity allocation. While 
we remain constructive on equities over the long term, a 
growing share of market returns has been driven by a relatively 
narrow group of AI -related beneficiaries. For investors, t he 
question is, therefore, not whether to maintain exposure to AI, 
but whether their portfolios have become overly dependent on 
a single theme or underlying risk factor.

Asset Allocation insights: Assessing

Hannah Chew

### Page 8

Global Market Outlook 8

US equities exhibit some of the highest levels of AI intensity, 
reflecting the dominant role US companies play across the AI 
value chain. By contrast, fixed income, private assets and gold 
display significantly lower AI sensitivity and derive their 
returns from different economic drivers. 
The objective is not to eliminate AI exposure. We continue to 
believe AI will remain one of the defining structural growth 
themes of the coming decade. Instead, the objective is to 
ensure portfolios participate in this opportunity without 
becoming excessively dependent on it. 
Stronger diversification through different 
return drivers 
One of the most common misconceptions is that greater 
diversification inevitably leads to lower returns. Our analysis 
suggests the opposite may be true when diversification is 
achieved through genuinely different return drivers. Both 
Foundation and Foundation + portfolios exhibit attractive risk-
return characteristics relative to a traditional 60/40 allocation 
(see Fig. 9). They deliver comparable or higher return 
potential with lower volatility, smaller drawdowns and reduced 
sensitivity to global equity markets.
